DELL — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • Consumer was 25.8% of CSG in FY2022, 13.6% in FY2026 and 10.9% in Q1 FY2027. Benchmarking this segment against consumer PC data misstates roughly 86% of it.
  • ISG gross margin declined 1,220 basis points between FY2024 and FY2026 and a further 690 basis points in Q1 FY2027, a total of 1,910 basis points. Operating margin over the same span declined 210 basis points.
  • ISG operating expense was $8,985M, $8,656M, $8,662M and $8,693M across FY2023 to FY2026, a range of 3.8% around the mean, while segment revenue rose 58.6%.
  • CSG gross margin declined 258 basis points from FY2024 to FY2026 while the operating-expense rate declined 54 basis points, so cost control offset about a fifth of the gross-margin decline and the rest went to the operating line.
  • Solving FY2024 against FY2026 gives an AI-server gross margin of 5.3% and a core rate of 40.1%; solving FY2025 against FY2026 gives 5.4% and 40.1%. The two independent pairs agree to within 10 basis points.
  • Set against a derived gross margin of 5%–8%, the operating income rate and the gross margin are approximately the same number, which implies almost no operating expense is loaded onto the line in Dell's internal reporting.
  • Dell earned $68 of segment operating profit per PC shipped in FY2026, $53 in Q4 FY2026 and $113 in Q1 FY2027. A $50 movement in cost per unit that is not recovered in price is most of the segment's profit.
  • One customer accounted for 12% of consolidated net revenue for the year ended 2026-01-30, with substantially all of it attributable to ISG, against no 10% customer in either FY2025 or FY2024
  • There are no disclosed escalators, indexation clauses or take-or-pay terms in Dell's customer contracts; the pass-through is negotiated quote by quote.
  • Services was 45.6% of FY2026 consolidated gross profit at a 44.8% gross margin, against 13.7% on product, and services revenue declined from $24,147M to $23,133M while product revenue grew 27%.
capital-allocation
  • It was initiated at $0.33 a quarter in Q1 FY2023 and raised 12%, 20%, 18% and 20% since, to $0.630 a quarter for FY2027, compounding at 17.5% a year against a stated commitment of 10% or more, first set through FY2028 and extended through FY2030.
competitive-advantage
  • ISG holds a real competitive advantage in cost and distribution.
  • ISG produced an operating margin between 9.9% and 13.2% in every one of the last nine fiscal years, including a year when segment revenue declined 12% and a year when it grew 40%.
  • Working from management's stated mid-single-digit operating income rate for AI-optimized servers, the non-AI half of ISG earns roughly 16%–18% while AI-optimized servers earn about 5%.
  • AI-optimized servers were 6% of ISG revenue in FY2024, 41% in FY2026 and 56% in Q1 FY2027. Storage, which management repeatedly identifies as the largest single source of ISG profitability, went from 48.0% of ISG revenue in FY2024 to 27.3% in FY2026 to 14.9% in Q1 FY2027.
  • Switching costs are real in storage: a PowerStore array is qualified into a customer's data-protection and compliance workflow and the data on it is expensive to migrate, while a rack of GB300s is a substitutable compute block bought against a competitive quote
  • The fastest-growing half of ISG earns a lower operating margin than both the ODM and the pure-play assembler it competes with, and that adverse gap is widening. Every other gap is favourable to Dell and several are widening in Dell's favour. The advantage is intact in the businesses that are shrinking as a share of revenue and absent in the business that is growing.
  • ISG earns excess returns today and is defensible for now. It is open to erosion as the mix shifts toward a product line where Dell has no demonstrated cost or price advantage over its rivals and where the scarce inputs and the pricing power belong to NVIDIA and the memory suppliers.
  • HP Inc. is the closest match on shape: the same direct-model procurement scale, configure-to-order manufacturing, and returns generated by negative operating capital
  • CSG's advantage is a distribution and procurement scale position in branded PCs. It is real but shallow: it produces no pricing power, no margin premium over its nearest branded rival, and a margin deficit to the unit-scale leader.
  • CSG added $2,591M of revenue in FY2026 and lost $139M of operating income doing it, a negative incremental margin.
  • Dell CSG's operating-margin premium over HP Personal Systems was −5bp in CY2021, +162bp in CY2023, −8bp in CY2024 and +23bp in CY2025, a mean near +43bp with the sign flipping.
  • Lenovo, the unit-scale leader at 24.4% share against Dell's 13.6%, earned a 7.2% operating margin in its Intelligent Devices Group on $58.9B of revenue growing 17% in the year ended 2026-03-31, against Dell CSG's 5.6% on $51.0B growing 5%.
  • Dell's own COO said at the October 2025 analyst meeting that Dell has lost share in the 75% of PC units outside the premium band and committed to re-entering those categories while holding a 5%–7% operating margin.
  • Every branded PC vendor earns a high accounting return because none owns the factories and all are financed by suppliers and deferred revenue. In this industry the metric measures capital structure, not competitive advantage.
competitive-landscape
  • Dell is the scale leader in every branded line ISG competes in and the gap widened sharply over the last four quarters, but almost entirely in accelerated servers, where the margin is thinnest.
  • Dell's largest competitor is ODM Direct, which booked $61,538M in CY1Q26, 50.2% of all server revenue and 3.0x Dell's IDC-tracked server revenue, roughly the size of the entire branded pool.
  • And in the mainstream x86 market where Dell earns most of its ISG profit, Dell's own deck shows share of 29% in 2025 against a 34% peak in 2022, five points lost over three years on a metric management presents as a decade of gains.
  • Super Micro is holding position at roughly half Dell's scale while pricing more aggressively: its gross margin declined from 13.8% in FY2024 to 11.1% in FY2025, which its own 10-K attributes to a strategy of offering competitive pricing to gain market share.
  • Dell holds more share than the next four vendors combined, 31.2% against 30.9%.
  • Both are true because Dell's reported line is much broader than the tracker: it includes hyperconverged systems, data-protection appliances, software-defined storage, third-party resale and attached services.
  • Dell's share is highest in the smallest categories: 41% of purpose-built backup appliances and 33% of high-end, against 23% of midrange, which is roughly 65% of the market.
  • Dell holds 28% of commercial premium, roughly 27% of industry units growing at a 7% unit compound rate. Dell holds 9% of the other 73% of the market, which is shrinking at −1% a year and where it has lost about six points of share since CY2018-19.
  • Apple takes more operating profit out of the industry from 25.6 million units than Dell and HP take from 98.5 million combined.
expectations
  • Dell's own long-term framework implies FY2030 revenue of $148,825–160,268M; Bloomberg consensus is $235,734M, 47% higher, and management declined to revise the framework when asked directly on 2026-05-28.
  • Either the framework is re-based at the next analyst meeting, or Dell's own FY2027 guidance implies flat-to-declining revenue from FY2028 through FY2030.
financial-history
  • Operating expenses declined from $18,273M in FY2020 to $14,558M in FY2026, and in every year since FY2022, a $3,715M reduction while revenue grew $28,723M, taking operating expense from 21.5% of revenue to 12.8%.
forward-model
  • The decline is the reversal of memory-driven average-selling-price inflation: base-case traditional-server ASP gives back roughly half of its FY2027 gain over FY2029–FY2030, and CSG revenue per unit gives back most of its FY2027 gain over FY2028–FY2029.
  • Base-case non-GAAP operating income is $15,585M in FY2027 and $16,545M in FY2032, a 1.0% annual change across a period in which revenue swings from $167B to $184B and back to $180B. Pass-through revenue enters at close to cost, so when it leaves it takes little profit with it.
history-inflections
  • The ISG 14% operating margin target for FY2023 was approached once, at 13.2%, and the segment has been below it in every year since, at 11.7% in FY2026. The company-level framework has been reset three times, in 2021, 2023 and 2025, with no bridge from the prior one.
management-claims
  • Dell spends more on engineering per revenue dollar than either and earns less on the product where the engineering is supposed to differentiate. The advantage shows up in units and share, which is what a distribution advantage produces.
management-incentives
  • The company's public framework, set at the October 2025 analyst meeting, commits to 15%-plus non-GAAP diluted EPS growth, 100%-plus net income to adjusted free cash flow conversion, and returning 80%-plus of adjusted free cash flow.
risks
  • At management's stated mid-single-digit rate, $10B of AI revenue produces roughly $1.24B less operating income than $10B of non-AI ISG revenue.
secular-trends
  • Dell tells four secular stories: AI infrastructure, a traditional-server refresh, a PC refresh, and the enterprise data explosion. Measured against the last ten years of results, only the first shows up in the numbers.
  • Dell's server-revenue share gain is the largest in the industry: 6.3% of worldwide server revenue in Q1 CY2025 to 16.5% in Q1 CY2026, and 17.4% to 33.2% of the branded pool.
  • Memory inflation raises revenue at close to cost, so it depresses the gross-margin rate while leaving operating expense untouched, and it destroys unit demand at the same time.
  • Dell is at the top of a price cycle superimposed on the first two years of a volume cycle in one product line.
technology
  • Dell is not differentiated at the silicon, architecture or platform layer, and the filings say so.
  • Dell is level with HP Inc. and Super Micro, at roughly one-sixth of Cisco's intensity.
  • This is the one place where Dell owns defensible technology, and it is where the profit comes from.
  • L11 and L12 are standard industry validation tiers that Super Micro markets in identical language [web], so Dell is describing an industry tier at which it claims to be faster.
  • The advantages that are visible in the financials are non-technical: supply-chain scale and long-term supplier agreements, factory rack integration and field deployment services, a direct sales force covering the largest enterprise and public-institution accounts, and Dell Financial Services.
value-chain
  • On the identical end-customer dollar, NVIDIA captures roughly $0.56–0.64 of gross profit against Dell's $0.05–0.08. Dell is the layer with the customer relationship, the credit exposure and the inventory risk.
  • Two separate constraints bind, and Dell owns neither. NVIDIA allocation limits AI-optimized servers, which are $60B of the $167B FY2027 guide, and Dell does not buy the HBM inside NVIDIA's trays.
  • Dell's supply position in this shortage is relationship- and scale-based, and its forward commitment book is far smaller as a share of revenue than NVIDIA's.
  • Stripping AI servers out at a 7.5% assumed gross margin, non-AI product gross margin moved from 14.9% to 18.4%, an expansion of about 350 basis points during the worst memory-cost inflation on record.
  • Roughly 61% of the raise is in lines the 10-Q attributes primarily to average selling price against flat-to-declining units.

Consumer was 25.8% of CSG in FY2022, 13.6% in FY2026 and 10.9% in Q1 FY2027. Benchmarking this segment against consumer PC data misstates roughly 86% of it.

presentationqualifies
On the fix and consumer, it's pretty darn simple. We need to improve profitability. We need to have more products to cover the marketplace. So more products, the right product at the right price, in the right go-to-market, whether that's dell.com, whether it's in a retail store or whether that's through the channel, right product, right time, readily available, some marketing behind it to tell people we're in it to win it. presentationqualifies
However, in the PC business, it is a business of scale and 75% of the units aren't in the 25, and it's the area where we have lost share. And they are very important categories in this area that we should be in, that we have not been in aggressive enough. And my message to you today is you should expect us to be in the categories that matter in the 75% that we have lost share in. You might ask what are some of those? Clearly, there's the premium consumer business. But more importantly, there's the education market. There's the lower price band or emerging commercial PCs across our industry.

filingsupports
The transaction price reflects the amount of consideration to which the Company expects to be entitled in exchange for transferring goods or services to the customer. If the consideration promised in a contract includes a variable amount, the Company estimates the amount to which it expects to be entitled using either the expected value or most likely amount method. Generally, volume discounts, rebates, and sales returns reduce the transaction price. In determining the transaction price, the Company only includes amounts that are not subject to significant future reversal.
— Dell Technologies Inc. Form 10-K, Fiscal 2026 (year ended January 30, 2026), 2026-03-16

Peer: Corning Incorporated

websupports
“The typical categories that we see are L10, where it's just a basic mechanical assembly, including racks, your rails, and power cooling. L11 takes it to that next level by integrating the optical and network cabling as well. L11 is critical in that sense, because it addresses some of the main pain points of AI deployments, which includes the cabling density, reducing errors and making sure it's very serviceable on site without locking customers into any specific compute platforms.”
— Sarah Koogler, market development manager, HSDC, Corning Incorporated, DatacenterDynamics, 'Rack integration 101: What L11 really means for AI data centers', 2026-04-02
websupports
As Koogler explains, L11 is our main focus, but you can go a stage further – L12 takes things to another level, where instead of having in-rack integration, you have a multi rack cluster assembly that includes your compute and storage networking cabling between racks, not just within the rack, plus validation of the software stack.
— DatacenterDynamics (editorial), summarising Sarah Koogler, DatacenterDynamics, 'Rack integration 101: What L11 really means for AI data centers', 2026-04-02

filingsupports
Because we generally maintain minimal levels of component and product inventories, a disruption in component or product availability could harm our ability to fill customer orders on a timely basis and at an acceptable price. The impact of supply constraints on our operations may be more acute during periods of rapid growth in demand for new products and services, such as the current demand for AI-optimized solutions.
— Dell Technologies 10-K, FY2026, 2026-03-16
filingsupports
Our efficient supply chain management allows us to enter into flexible and mutually beneficial purchase arrangements with our suppliers in order to minimize inventory risk. … These purchase orders are typically fulfilled within 30 days and are entered into during the ordinary course of business in order to establish best pricing and continuity of supply for our production. Purchase orders are not included in purchase obligations, as they typically represent our authorization to purchase rather than binding purchase obligations.
— Dell Technologies 10-K, FY2026, 2026-03-16
filingsupports
As of May 1, 2026, we had purchase obligations of $20.8 billion, of which $17.3 billion is payable within twelve months.
— Dell Technologies 10-Q, Q1 FY2027, 2026-06-09
filingcontradicts
Purchase obligations exclude contracts that may be canceled without penalty, open purchase orders that are entered into during the ordinary course of business, and certain portions of strategic supplier agreements. … Strategic supplier agreements are typically long-term in nature with fixed or minimum quantities to be purchased from our suppliers throughout the term of the contract in order to minimize inventory risk, particularly in a dynamic commodity supply environment. The portions of strategic supplier agreements for which certain commodities will be purchased at a price to be mutually agreed upon after the balance sheet date cannot be reasonably estimated and therefore are excluded from purchase obligations.
— Dell Technologies 10-Q, Q1 FY2027, 2026-06-09
filingsupports
To meet growing demand, we have increased our purchases of certain components with suppliers, resulting in increased purchase obligations. We expect our purchases of those components will continue to increase, including committed purchases under long-term strategic supplier agreements at prices to be mutually agreed upon at a future date.
— Dell Technologies 10-Q, Q1 FY2027, 2026-06-09

Peer: Micron Technology

filingsupports
We recently executed certain strategic customer agreements, including agreements executed subsequent to May 28, 2026. These agreements include binding commitments for specific volumes over the multi-year contract terms. Strategic customer agreements often include substantial customer deposits and related financial commitments. In connection with these strategic customer agreements, we expect to receive cash deposits and related financial commitments of $22 billion for agreements concluded to date. Approximately $18 billion of these commitments will be in the form of cash deposits.
— Micron Technology 10-Q, Q3 FY2026 (quarter ended 2026-05-28), 2026-06-25
filingsupports
Strategic customer agreements are structured as take-or-pay agreements, with binding commitments for specific volumes over the multi-year contract terms. Pricing for most agreements is either fixed, or is subject to minimum and maximum pricing. The largest agreements generally have a ceiling price for existing products that approximates the market price in the second calendar quarter of 2026, and a floor price through the term of the agreement.
— Micron Technology 10-Q, Q3 FY2026, 2026-06-25
filingsupports
We expect gross margins from our strategic customer agreements with price bands, even at floor pricing levels, to yield gross margins well above our peak quarterly margins in any past cycle. Accordingly, we believe these agreements accelerate the transformation of our business model and will significantly enhance the durability and predictability of our financial performance.
— Micron Technology 10-Q, Q3 FY2026, 2026-06-25
